# Epigraphy translation scaffold — 100 inscriptions
# Under each [ENGLISH], type a faithful English translation of the Latin
# above it. Expand standard abbreviations (D(is) M(anibus), v(ixit) a(nnos),
# H(ic) s(itus) e(st), etc.). Free prose, one or more lines. Leave blank to skip.
# Then: python epigraphy_translate.py assemble _epi_translate_02000.txt

=== HD004930
# AE 1978, 0715 | 271 CE – 330 CE | funerary
[LATIN]
D(is) M(anibus)
Ulpiae Aureliae Va
leriae virgini dextra
t(a)e annis III mensibus
VIIII d(iebus) XVII filiae Aure
li Herculani v(iri) e(gregii) duce
nari qui vixit annis
XLII mens(ibus) VIII d(iebus) XVI
nepotiae pientissi
m(a)e Ulp(ius) Valerius Au
relianus v(ir) e(gregius) cente
narius et Titinia
Mansueta stola
ta femina viator
resiste et lege nihil ul
tra crudelius h(oc) m(onumento) c(ernere) po(tes)
[ENGLISH]
To the Spirits of the Dead. For Ulpia Aurelia Valeria, a girl betrothed, who lived 3 years, 9 months, and 17 days, daughter of Aurelius Herculanus, a man of distinction, a commander of a hundred, who himself lived 42 years, 8 months, and 16 days; and for a most devoted granddaughter — Ulpius Valerius Aurelianus, a man of distinction, a centurion, and Titinia Mansueta, a woman of the stoled class, set this up. Traveller, stop and read: nothing more cruel than this can you behold at this monument.

=== HD017227
# AE 1963, 0184 | 71 CE – 100 CE | funerary
[LATIN]
τὴν στήλην Τειμόθεος Νονίου Ι νήμης
χάριν·
Κορνηλία Φορτουνάτα ἡ κὲ Δουτοῦρος
χαῖρε·
τὴν Σταβυλίωνος σύνβιον Κορνηλίαν
ἀνάγνωθι Φορτουνᾶταν εὐλογῶν ξένε
στοργὴν πρὸς ἀνδρὸς κοσμίαν καὶ σώφρονα
διατελέσασαν συνπαθῆ τε δάκρυα
λιποῦσαν· εἶτα παρίθι καὶ ἐπιτύγχανε.
Cornelia Fortunata M(arci) Corneli
Stabilionis coniunx vixit an(nos) XXX
h(ic) s(ita) e(st)
Timotheus Noni f(ilius) d(e) s(uo) memoriae causa
[ENGLISH]
Timotheus son of Nonius set up this stele for the sake of remembrance. Cornelia Fortunata, also called Dutourus — farewell. Read, stranger, and bless the name of Cornelia Fortunata, wife of Stabilion: she remained devoted to her husband, orderly and chaste, and left behind tears of sympathy. Then pass on and fare well. Cornelia Fortunata, wife of Marcus Cornelius Stabilion, lived 30 years. Here she lies. Timotheus son of Nonius set this up at his own expense for the sake of her memory.

=== HD037225
# AE 1906, p. 34 s. n. 106 | 180 CE – 183 CE | public
[LATIN]
Imp(erator) Caes(ar) M(arcus) Aur(elius) Commodus
Antoninus Aug(ustus) Pius Sarm(aticus) Germ(anicus)
pont(ifex) max(imus) trib(unicia) pot(estate) VI imp(erator) IIII co(n)s(ul) IIII p(ater) p(atriae)
ripam omnem burgis a solo extructis
item praesidi(i)s per loca opportuna
ad clandestinos latrunculorum
transitus oppositis munivit
per L(ucium) Cornelium
Felicem Plotianum
leg(atum) pr(o) pr(aetore)
[ENGLISH]
The Emperor Caesar Marcus Aurelius Commodus Antoninus Augustus Pius, Sarmaticus, Germanicus, chief priest, with tribunician power for the sixth time, acclaimed victor four times, consul four times, father of the fatherland, fortified the entire riverbank by constructing watchtowers from the ground up, and likewise by positioning garrisons at strategic locations to counter the clandestine crossings of raiders — all of this through Lucius Cornelius Felix Plotianus, his legate acting as propraetor.

=== HD023857
# AE 1928, 0178 | unknown | public
[LATIN]
Imp(eratori) Tito Caes(ari) divi Vespas(iani)
f(ilio) Vespas(iano) Aug(usto) p(ontifici) m(aximo) tr(ibunicia) pot(estate)
VIIII imp(eratori) XV p(atri) p(atriae) co(n)s(uli) VIII
Caes(ari) divi Vespas(iani) f(ilio) Domi
tiano co(n)s(uli) VII via nova
facta ab Asturica Bracar(augusta)
C(aio) Calp(etano) Rant(io) Quir(inale) Valerio
[Festo leg(ato) Aug(usti) pro pr(aetore)]
m(ilia) p(assuum) XXIII
[ENGLISH]
To the Emperor Titus Caesar Vespasianus Augustus, son of the deified Vespasian, chief priest, with tribunician power for the ninth time, acclaimed victor fifteen times, father of the fatherland, consul for the eighth time; and to Caesar Domitianus, son of the deified Vespasian, consul for the seventh time. A new road constructed from Asturica to Bracara Augusta under Gaius Calpetanus Rantius Quirinalis Valerius Festus, legate of Augustus acting as propraetor. 23 miles.

=== HD000862
# AE 1983, 0975 | 251 CE – 300 CE | funerary
[LATIN]
…
… CA()
CIBOS() NAO()
agricolae in [spl(endidissima)]
re p(ublica) Bihensi Bilt[a]
conductori pari
atori restitutori
fundi Aufidiani et
praeter cetera bona q[uae]
in eodem f(undo) fecit steriles
qu[o]que oleastri surculo[s]
inserendo plurimas o[leas]
instituit puteum [iuxta]
viam pomarium cum tric[hilis]
post collectarium vin[eas]
novellas sub silva aequ[e in]
stituit uxor mari[to]
incomparabili fec[it]
[ENGLISH]
… a farmer in the most illustrious community of Biha Bilta, a leaseholder, equestrian, restorer of the Aufidian estate, who — beyond the other improvements he made on that estate — by grafting cuttings onto barren wild olive trees established very many productive olives, and likewise laid out a well beside the road, an orchard with garden arbours, a collection cistern, and new vineyards beneath the woodland. His wife set this up for an incomparable husband.

=== HD017875
# AE 1961, 0181 | 101 CE – 200 CE | defixio
[LATIN]
Secundina Mercurio et
Moltino mandat ut si quis |(denarios) XIIII
sive draucus duos sustulit ut
eum sive fortunas eius in[fi]
dus Cacus sic auferat quo
modi sic ill(a)e ablatum est [i]d quod
vobis delegat ut persecuatis sic
vobisque deligat ut
Persicuatis sic et eum
aversum a fortunis (s)u
is avertatis et a suis prox
simis sic et ab eis quos caris
simos (h)abeat h[o]c vobis
mandat vos cor
[ipi]atis …
…
[ENGLISH]
Secundina charges Mercury and Moltinus that whoever stole 14 denarii or two armlets, that the treacherous Cacus take away that person or his goods, in the same manner as those things were taken from her. This she delegates to you, that you pursue it. And to you she entrusts the task of pursuing it: turn him away from his own fortunes, and from his nearest kin, and from those he holds most dear. This she charges you — may you seize him … …

=== HD027768
# AE 1949, 0201 | 238 CE – 249 CE | public
[LATIN]
Marti Gradivo quem aput sic leg(ionem) VII Cl(audiam)
tempore quo ad eradendum nomen
saevissimae dominationis
missus cum vexillationes
Moesiae inferioris voltus hh(ostium) pp(ublicorum)
[de vexillis et can]tabris
[ultro detra]here nollent
[auctores im]prosperi conatus
[coercerat] congressione
[facta prima] in acie constitutus
[invocave]rat Clodius Celsinus
[signum quod in be]llo voverat … [ posuit]
[ENGLISH]
To Mars Gradivus. Clodius Celsinus, who was with the Seventh Claudian Legion at the time when, sent to eradicate the name of a most savage tyranny, and having assembled the vexillation detachments of Lower Moesia — when the faces of the public enemies refused of their own accord to pull down the standards and battle-flags — had restrained the ringleaders of the ill-starred attempt, and having joined battle had taken his stand in the front line of the field, had invoked this god; he set up the votive sign which he had vowed in the war.

Celtiber
ordine turbato maior sepelire min[o]rem

muta[ … ]is spatio quo … I …
[t]e qua morte ratis … VT C …
unius et pueri [ … ]OT …
… VAE PROPERAS[ … … ]C …
… DC …
…
…
[ENGLISH]
Quintus Cassius, son of Niger, a Celtiberian. The natural order was overturned: the elder was compelled to bury the younger, and to lay him to rest far from his homeland, far from the citadel … lost … snatched away by death, nor … it would have been gentler in the homeland … you alone were dear to me … there you were a mother … [The remaining verses are too fragmentary to translate fully.]

=== HD031384
# B.E. Thomasson, Fasti Africani. Senatorische und ritterliche Amtsträger in den römischen Provinzen Nordafrikas von Augus | 164 CE | honourific
[LATIN]
[Imp(eratori) Caesari L(ucio) Aurelio] Vero Armeniaco Aug(usto) divi An[tonini Pii filio]
[divi Hadriani nepoti divi T]raiani Parthici pronepot[i divi Nervae adnepoti]
[pont(ifici) max(imo) imp(eratori) II tribun(icia) p]otest(ate) IIII consuli II pro[consuli dedicante]
[C(aio) Maesio Picatiano leg(ato) Augustorum] pro pra[et]ore pa[trono coloniae d(ecreto) d(ecurionum) p(ecunia) p(ublica)]
[ENGLISH]
To the Emperor Caesar Lucius Aurelius Verus Armeniacus Augustus, son of the deified Antoninus Pius, grandson of the deified Hadrian, great-grandson of the deified Trajan Parthicus, great-great-grandson of the deified Nerva, chief priest, acclaimed victor twice, with tribunician power for the fourth time, consul twice, proconsul — dedicated under the supervision of Gaius Maesius Picatianus, legate of the Emperors acting as propraetor, patron of the colony — by decree of the decurions at public expense.

=== HD032542
# M. Horster, Bauinschriften römischer Kaiser (Stuttgart 2001) 87. 272- 273, Nr. Ia 11, 7 | 138 CE | public
[LATIN]
Imp(erator) Caes(ar) divi Hadriani fil(ius) divi Traiani Parthici nep(os) divi N[ervae]
pronepos T(itus) Aelius Hadrianus Antoninus Aug(ustus) Pius pontif(ex) max(imus) trib(unicia) potes[tat(e) II co(n)s(ul)]
thermas in quarum exstructionem divos pater suus HS XX(viginties centena milia) pollic[itus erat]
adiecta pecunia quanto ampliuus desiderabatur item marmoribus ad omnem c[ultum fecit]
[ENGLISH]
The Emperor Caesar Titus Aelius Hadrianus Antoninus Augustus Pius, son of the deified Hadrian, grandson of the deified Trajan Parthicus, great-grandson of the deified Nerva, chief priest, with tribunician power for the second time, consul — the baths, for the construction of which his deified father had pledged 2,000,000 sesterces, he completed, adding as much further money as was needed, and likewise provided marble for every decoration.

[ENGLISH]
Marcus Aurelius …us, standard-bearer of the First Cavalry Wing of Lance-bearers, Antoniniana, and for Elvia Bona, his dearest wife; and for his daughter Aurelia Valerina; and for Aelia Sabina; and for Elvius Verecundus, aged 70; for Pulchra Sura, aged 60; and for Elvius Vitalis, aged 18; and for Aurelius Masuetianus, aged 7; and for Aelius Floridus — Marcus Aurelius Valerianus set this up while still living, for himself and his family.

=== HD042932
# AE 1987, 0902 | 341 CE – 344 CE | honourific
[LATIN]
[P]acifico piissimo[q]ue p(ri)ncip[i]
d(omino) n(ostro) Fl(avio) Cl(audio) Constanti victor[i a]
[c] triumfatori [p]erpetuo Au[g(usto)]
Ant(onius) Marcellinus et Dom(itius) Leonti[us e]
t Fab(ius) Titianus vv(iri) cc(larissimi) praeff(ecti) praet(orio)
n(umini) m(aiestati)q(ue) eorum semper devotissim[i]
[p]rocurante Palladio v(iro) p(erfectissimo) praesid[e]
[p]rovinciae Thraciae
consecraverunt
[ENGLISH]
To the most peaceful and most devoted ruler, our lord Flavius Claudius Constantius, ever-victorious and ever-triumphant Augustus — Antonius Marcellinus, Domitius Leontius, and Fabius Titianus, men of clarissimate rank, praetorian prefects, ever most devoted to his divine power and majesty, dedicated this, under the supervision of Palladius, a man of perfectissimate rank, governor of the province of Thrace.

=== HD044382
# ILBulg 325 | 201 CE – 250 CE | funerary
[LATIN]
Θ(εοῖς) Κ(αταχθονίοις)
οὔνομά μοι
Βασιλεύς
κεῖμαι δ' ἅμα
μητρὶ θανού
σῃ μοίρῃ ὑπ' ἀρ
γαλέῃ προδαμεὶς
νούσῳ τε βαρείῃ
Κυζίκιος δέ με θά
ψεν ἀγακλείτη τε
Σεκούνδα πρῶ
τον ὑπὲρ γενύων
ἄνθεα δρεψάμενον
D(is) M(anibus)
monte sub hoc tegito[r]
Basileus ecce s[e]pul
tus | precipiti leto iuen
is iuvenis fatorum lege [p]eracta
quem genuit mater mecum
iacet i[ps]a sepulta Kyzici
o mihi factus honos
simul atque Secunda
[ENGLISH]
To the Gods of the Underworld. My name is Basileus. I lie here together with my mother who died, cut down before my time by harsh fate and heavy illness. Kyzikios and the renowned Secunda buried me, who had only just begun to pluck the first flowers above my cheeks. To the Spirits of the Dead. Beneath this mound lies Basileus, buried here, a young man struck down by sudden death when fate's law was fulfilled; the mother who bore him lies buried here with me. The honour was paid to me by Kyzikios and Secunda together.

Dedicated on the day before the Kalends of September, in the consulship of our lord the Emperor Gordianus Augustus for the second time and Pompeianus.

The last threads of the Fates closed off the day of wretched Principius — a boy whom his grandfather and father had entrusted to distinguished military service and committed to the care of his paternal uncle to be nurtured, just when he was first beginning to take on the years of young manhood with beautiful down upon his cheeks, as they looked upon him as a great future pillar of the fatherland. Alas, the wretches who had boasted to themselves of a joyful old age — Ravenna sent back to their house a cruel grief. These wretches have marked their own sorrow with this inscription.

in fide Xr(ist)i annos LXV migrabit sic ab

D(omi)ni mil(les)imo XXX[X] indictio I audi
at bocem sic D(omi)ni et resurgat
in bita sic aeterna am(en)
[ENGLISH]
In the name of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Spirit, amen. In this tomb rests the body of the servant of Christ, Sisinnus, son of Firmus the reader. May he have eternal rest. He lived in the faith of Christ 65 years and departed from this world on the twenty-first day before the Kalends of June in the year of the Lord one thousand and forty, in the first indiction. May he hear the voice of the Lord and rise to eternal life. Amen.

Sacred to the Spirits of the Dead. Melitine, aged 9 years, 6 months, and 8 days — here lies this child, to be mourned by her father through the ages; her mother, consumed by unceasing grief, seeks her who was snatched away. Charming and sweet … the sort of child everyone would wish to rear for himself — yet the year ten robbed her of the gift of light. Whoever reads of this fate, let him curse the unjust destiny. May the earth lie lightly upon you. Carpophorus, father, and Felicula, mother, for their most devoted daughter.

=== HD018030
# AE 1956, 0122 | unknown | public

[ENGLISH]
Sacred to the Spirits of the Dead. Here is Vincentius, the glory of pantomime dancers, living forever on the lips of the common people — beloved by all not merely for the art at which stage performers excel, but because he was a man of probity and goodness, blameless and self-restrained in all things, who, whenever he danced his roles, held the theatre spellbound from dawn until evening. Buried here, he now has the city walls as his dwelling. He lived 23 years in his prime; yet in his holiness of life and grace of movement he was more eloquent still.

Good, holy Attis, lord — be present, come, angered against Liberalis. === HD037944
# J.M. Abascal Palazón - S.F. Ramallo Asensio, La ciudad de Carthago nova: La documentación epigráfica (Murcia 1997) 474-4 | 30 BCE – 14 CE | funerary
[LATIN]
[Q(uintus) Lusius] L(uci) f(ilius) Seni[ca]
[mollem robusteis n]ondum formata iuvent[us]
[aetatem Lusi vi]ribus induerat
[cum carae exoptans] complexum saepe so[roris]
[multa viae dum volt millia conficere]
[caeditur infesto concursu forte latronum]
[sic rapit hoc clades corpus acerba nimis]
[illa aetas credo hoc tribuit tempore mortis]
[ut bona non neminit seic mala ne timeat]
[ENGLISH]
Quintus Lusius Senica, son of Lucius. His youth, not yet fully formed, had clothed the gentle age of Lusus in strong vigour; while longing often for the embrace of his dear sister and wishing to cover many miles of road, he was cut down in a fierce clash with a band of brigands. Thus a cruel disaster snatched away this body far too soon. That age, I believe, granted him this gift at the hour of death: that, having known no good fortune, he need fear no misfortune.

=== HD037805
# J.M. Abascal Palazón - S.F. Ramallo Asensio, la ciudad de Carthago Nova: la documentación epigráfica (Murcia 1997) 405-4 | 30 BCE – 30 CE | funerary
[LATIN]
Sicinia C(ai) f(ilia) Secunda
filia cum matre est hospes sei forte requiris
heic sita quas rapuit mortis acerva dies
sed prius eripuit matri qui in omnia pollet
crudelis cassus filiolam e manibus paene inmatura
morte ereptam sibi gnatan heu quantum mater [fleverit]
indiciost nam postquam [fletu] et monumento hoc conde
coravit gnatam per luctus reddidit ipsa animam
[ENGLISH]
Sicinia Secunda, daughter of Gaius. Daughter and mother are here together, stranger, if by chance you ask — laid here, both snatched away by the bitter day of death. But first, cruel Chance — which holds sway over all things — tore away the little daughter from the mother's arms, taken almost before her time; ah, how greatly the mother must have wept for her daughter stolen from her! The proof is this: for after she had adorned her daughter with weeping and with this monument, she herself gave up her soul amid her grief.

She lived 18 years; she departed in peace on the day before the Ides of June in the year of the province 376.

Bitter grief will torment me as long as I live, because my child was snatched from me by a wretched death. By sudden calamity youthful life collapsed, and the final day robbed her of the light. Sad and wretched, I constantly question the divine powers: why, I ask, do they not quickly destroy me too? Now that you, dear Classicus, lie buried in the tomb, may the Earth cover your bones and be forever kindly to you.

Whoever you are, pause and contemplate our tomb as you approach; I beg you, read briefly my fate. My mother bore me, nurtured me at her breast, and had set forth the labor, the hope, and the support of her own life. Here I lie, unhappy ash and burned cinder; here the threads were drawn on the spinning-wheel in discord. Cease now, dear little mother, to weep for me, and allow us to lie henceforth in one place together. … Rufus.

=== HD029748
# AE 1910, 0100 | 151 CE – 200 CE | funerary
[LATIN]
Agirro Epicadi f(ilio) principi k(astelli) Salthua
et Temeiae Glavi f(iliae) fecit Epicadus f(ilius)
ut primum aetatis complerunt tempora vitae
et genitor fato conditus est tumulo
protinus inscripsit pietas nomen matrisque patrisq(ue)
ut legerent cuncti genitoris nomina saepe
hoc decet ut nati componant ossa parentum
et cineri sedem substituat pietas
[ENGLISH]
For Agirrus, son of Epicadus, chief of the fort of Salthua, and for Temeia, daughter of Glavus, Epicadus the son made this. As soon as they had completed the span of their lives and his father was laid in the tomb by fate, filial devotion immediately inscribed the names of both mother and father, so that all might often read the father's name. It is right that children should gather the bones of their parents, and that devotion should provide a resting place for their ashes.

# L. Bricault, Recueil des inscriptions concernant les cultes Isiaques (RICIS) (Paris 2005) 722, Nr. 615/0401 | 101 CE – 200 CE | funerary

L(ucio) Cassio Hermo
doro nauclero
qui erat in colleg(io)
Serapis Salon(itano) per
freta per maria tra
iectus saepe per und(as)
qui non debuerat

[ENGLISH]
To Lucius Cassius Hermodorus, ship-master, who belonged to the college of Sarapis at Salona — one who crossed the straits, the seas, and the waves many a time, and who ought not to have met his end remaining in the Aternus. "But if, my husband, you were unwilling to live with me, would that I had accompanied you even to the eternal Styx, or on that funereal bark." Ulpia Candida, from Salona, set this up for her well-deserving husband.

set sic potius donis sollemnibus esse colend[um]
Surilla ego so(m) sic qu(a)e peremor sic filia C(h)loe[s]
[ENGLISH]
… they ordered … (it) not to be for her. Now (she) has a mother's devotion through the fields of the lord, and it was permitted for the mother to bury her grievous body; she who now has an inscription — not one lying in hostile earth, but rather to be honoured with solemn offerings. I am Surilla, who am destroyed, daughter of Chloe.

C(aius) Billienius Vitalis
f(aciendum) c(uravit)
[ENGLISH]
Gaius Cornelius Verus, son of Gaius, of the Pomptine voting tribe, from Dertona, veteran of the Second Legion Adiutrix, settled in the Ulpian Trajanic Colony of Poetovio on an agricultural discharge for the second time, served as beneficiarius of the consular legate, aged fifty years. Here lies. He ordered this to be made by his will. His heir, Gaius Billienius Vitalis, had (this) made.

=== HD068118
# AE 2006, 0820 | unknown | funerary
[LATIN]
Diis sic Manibus
et qu[ieti ae]tern(ae)
Facun[dinia]e Ger
manae quae vixit
annis XVIII mensib(us)
II diebus XVI cuius
anima et species
simul et aetas dul
cius melle fuit
memoriam quam solam defunctae
praestar(e) potui titu
lum litteris honoravi
coniugi karissimae
C(aius) Secundin(ius) Hylas et
sibi viv(u)s f(ecit) et sub a(scia) ded(icavit)
[ENGLISH]
To the Spirits of the Dead and to eternal rest: for Facundinia Germana, who lived eighteen years, two months, and sixteen days, whose soul and beauty alike, and her youth, were sweeter than honey. The memorial that I alone could offer to the deceased I have honoured with an inscription. To his most beloved wife, Gaius Secundinius Hylas made (this) also for himself while still alive and dedicated it under the adze.
